=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string s and a pattern string p, where p contains exactly two '*' characters.
The '*' in p matches any sequence of zero or more characters.
Return the length of the shortest substring in s that matches p. If there is no such substring, return -1.
Note: The empty substring is considered valid.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "abaacbaecebce", p = "ba*c*ce"
Output: 8
Explanation:
The shortest matching substring of p in s is "baecebce".

Example 2:

Input: s = "baccbaadbc", p = "cc*baa*adb"
Output: -1
Explanation:
There is no matching substring in s.

Example 3:

Input: s = "a", p = "**"
Output: 0
Explanation:
The empty substring is the shortest matching substring.

Example 4:

Input: s = "madlogic", p = "*adlogi*"
Output: 6
Explanation:
The shortest matching substring of p in s is "adlogi".

 
Constraints:

1 <= s.length <= 10^5
2 <= p.length <= 10^5
s contains only lowercase English letters.
p contains only lowercase English letters and exactly two '*'.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def shortestMatchingSubstring(self, s: str, p: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's design KMP:

def find_occurrences(text, pat):
    if not pat: return []
    n=len(text); m=len(pat)
    if m > n: return []
    # prefix function
    pi=[0]*m
    j=0
    for i in range(1,m):
        while j>0 and pat[i]!=pat[j]:
            j=pi[j-1]
        if pat[i]==pat[j]:
            j+=1
        pi[i]=j
    res=[]
    j=0
    for i,ch in enumerate(text):
        while j>0 and ch != pat[j]:
            j=pi[j-1]
        if ch == pat[j]:
            j+=1
        if j == m:
            res.append(i-m+1)
            j=pi[j-1]
    return res

Need ensure if m=0 not called. Good.

INF = 10**18

def two_part_min(starts1, len1, starts2, len2):
    # starts1, starts2 sorted lists of occurrence starts for non-empty patterns.
    # Need min over x in starts1, y in starts2, y >= x + len1 of (y + len2 - x)
    # = len2 + min(y - x)
    # If no pair, INF.
    # Use two pointers: for each y, need latest x <= y - len1. Since lists sorted.
    # We can iterate y in starts2, maintain ptr in starts1 pointing to last x <= y - len1.
    # min_y_minus_x = min(y - starts1[ptr]).
    # But if no x for early y, ptr=-1.
    res=INF
    ptr=-1
    for y in starts2:
        limit = y - len1
        while ptr + 1 < len(starts1) and starts1[ptr+1] <= limit:
            ptr += 1
        if ptr >= 0:
            res = min(res, y - starts1[ptr])
    if res == INF: return INF
    return res + len2

def three_part_min(A, lenA, B, lenB, C, lenC):
    if not A or not B or not C: return INF
    res=INF
    ptrA=-1
    ptrC=0
    # advance ptrC to first >= B[0]+lenB maybe in loop
    for j in B:
        limitA = j - lenA
        while ptrA + 1 < len(A) and A[ptrA+1] <= limitA:
            ptrA += 1
        if ptrA < 0:
            # no A before this B; but later B larger, limitA larger, so A may become available. Continue? If no A for current j, can't compute; but need still advance ptrC? We can skip C? We can continue, but ptrC threshold also increases. Need maintain ptrC even if no A? We can update ptrC for each j regardless.
            pass
        limitC = j + lenB
        while ptrC < len(C) and C[ptrC] < limitC:
            ptrC += 1
        if ptrA >=0 and ptrC < len(C):
            res = min(res, C[ptrC] + lenC - A[ptrA])
    return res

B = occurrences(s, b) if b else []
C = occurrences(s, c) if c else []
la, lb, lc = len(a), len(b), len(c)

if la == 0 and lb == 0 and lc == 0:
    return 0

ans = INF

if la > 0:
    if lb > 0:
        if lc > 0:
            ans = min(ans, three_part_min(A, la, B, lb, C, lc))
        else:
            ans = min(ans, two_part_min(A, la, B, lb))
    else:
        if lc > 0:
            ans = min(ans, two_part_min(A, la, C, lc))
        else:
            if A:
                ans = min(ans, la)
else:
    # la == 0
    if lb > 0:
        if lc > 0:
            ans = min(ans, two_part_min(B, lb, C, lc))
        else:
            if B:
                ans = min(ans, lb)
    else:
        if lc > 0:
            if C:
                ans = min(ans, lc)
        else:
            # all empty
            ans = 0

return -1 if ans == INF else ans

Let's write code:

class Solution:
    def shortestMatchingSubstring(self, s: str, p: str) -> int:
        def kmp(text, pat):
            n = len(text)
            m = len(pat)
            if m == 0:
                return []
            if m > n:
                return []
            pi = [0] * m
            j = 0
            # local variables? 
            for i in range(1, m):
                while j > 0 and pat[i] != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j-1]
                if pat[i] == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                pi[i] = j
            res = []
            j = 0
            for i, ch in enumerate(text):
                while j > 0 and ch != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j-1]
                if ch == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                if j == m:
                    res.append(i - m + 1)
                    j = pi[j-1]
            return res

        parts = p.split('*')
        a, b, c = parts[0], parts[1], parts[2]
        la, lb, lc = len(a), len(b), len(c)
        A = kmp(s, a) if la else []
        B = kmp(s, b) if lb else []
        C = kmp(s, c) if lc else []

        INF = 10**18

        def two_part(starts1, len1, starts2, len2):
            if not starts1 or not starts2:
                return INF
            best = INF
            ptr = -1
            # local variables for speed
            s1 = starts1
            n1 = len(s1)
            for y in starts2:
                limit = y - len1
                while ptr + 1 < n1 and s1[ptr + 1] <= limit:
                    ptr += 1
                if ptr >= 0:
                    diff = y - s1[ptr]
                    if diff < best:
                        best = diff
            if best == INF:
                return INF
            return best + len2

        def three_part(startsA, lenA, startsB, lenB, startsC, lenC):
            if not startsA or not startsB or not startsC:
                return INF
            best = INF
            ptrA = -1
            ptrC = 0
            nA = len(startsA)
            nC = len(startsC)
            sA = startsA
            sC = startsC
            for j in startsB:
                limitA = j - lenA
                while ptrA + 1 < nA and sA[ptrA + 1] <= limitA:
                    ptrA += 1
                limitC = j + lenB
                while ptrC < nC and sC[ptrC] < limitC:
                    ptrC += 1
                if ptrA >= 0 and ptrC < nC:
                    length = sC[ptrC] + lenC - sA[ptrA]
                    if length < best:
                        best = length
            return best

        ans = INF
        if la == 0 and lb == 0 and lc == 0:
            return 0

        if la > 0:
            if lb > 0:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = three_part(A, la, B, lb, C, lc)
                else:
                    ans = two_part(A, la, B, lb)
            else:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = two_part(A, la, C, lc)
                else:
                    if A:
                        ans = la
        else:
            if lb > 0:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = two_part(B, lb, C, lc)
                else:
                    if B:
                        ans = lb
            else:
                if lc > 0:
                    if C:
                        ans = lc
                # else all empty handled

        return -1 if ans == INF else ans

For small s, p with two stars, brute force would enumerate all substrings and match wildcard. Our decomposition should match.

Let's prove decomposition:
p = a * b * c. A string t matches p iff there exist u,v such that t = a + u + b + v + c (where a,b,c literals, u/v any). Thus t starts with a (if a nonempty), contains b after a, contains c after b. The shortest substring of s matching p is min over positions of a,b,c in order of end(c)-start(a), with if a empty start at b/c etc. Our formulas.

Need maybe consider if a,b,c can be empty; if a empty, t = u + b + v + c. The shortest t for given b,c is b + v + c with u empty. If b empty too, t = u + v + c = any + c, shortest c. If c empty too, t = u + v = any, shortest empty. Good.

Now, let's think about if p = "a**b" (b empty between stars). Our model a='a', b='', c='b'. Pattern t = a + u + v + b = a + (u+v) + b. Shortest t containing a then b. Good.
If p = "a*b**c"? Wait exactly two stars? "a*b**c" has three stars? Actually a * b ** c = three? No, "**" two consecutive plus another? p contains exactly two stars, so not. Good.

Now, let's consider if p = "a**" (a, star, star, no c). Our model a='a', b='', c=''. t = a + u + v. Shortest a. Good.
If p = "**a" (a='', b='', c='a'). t = u + v + a. Shortest a. Good.
If p = "*a*" (a='', b='a', c=''). t = u + a + v. Shortest a. Good.

Now, let's think about if p = "a*b*" (a='a', b='b', c=''). t = a + u + b + v. Shortest substring containing a then b, ending at b. Our two_part A,B. Good.
If p = "*a*b" (a='', b='a', c='b'). t = u + a + v + b. Shortest substring containing a then b, starting at a. Our two_part B,C. Good.
If p = "a*b*c" (all nonempty). t = a + u + b + v + c. Shortest substring containing a,b,c in order. Good.

Now, let's think about if p="a**b" and s="baaab" but A at1
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def shortestMatchingSubstring(self, s: str, p: str) -> int:
        def kmp(text: str, pat: str):
            n, m = len(text), len(pat)
            if m == 0:
                return []
            if m > n:
                return []

            pi = [0] * m
            j = 0
            for i in range(1, m):
                while j > 0 and pat[i] != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j - 1]
                if pat[i] == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                pi[i] = j

            res = []
            j = 0
            for i, ch in enumerate(text):
                while j > 0 and ch != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j - 1]
                if ch == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                if j == m:
                    res.append(i - m + 1)
                    j = pi[j - 1]
            return res

        a, b, c = p.split('*')
        la, lb, lc = len(a), len(b), len(c)

        A = kmp(s, a) if la else []
        B = kmp(s, b) if lb else []
        C = kmp(s, c) if lc else []

        INF = 10**18

        def two_part(starts1, len1, starts2, len2):
            if not starts1 or not starts2:
                return INF
            best = INF
            ptr = -1
            n1 = len(starts1)
            for y in starts2:
                limit = y - len1
                while ptr + 1 < n1 and starts1[ptr + 1] <= limit:
                    ptr += 1
                if ptr >= 0:
                    best = min(best, y - starts1[ptr])
            return INF if best == INF else best + len2

        def three_part(startsA, lenA, startsB, lenB, startsC, lenC):
            if not startsA or not startsB or not startsC:
                return INF
            best = INF
            ptrA = -1
            ptrC = 0
            nA = len(startsA)
            nC = len(startsC)

            for j in startsB:
                limitA = j - lenA
                while ptrA + 1 < nA and startsA[ptrA + 1] <= limitA:
                    ptrA += 1

                limitC = j + lenB
                while ptrC < nC and startsC[ptrC] < limitC:
                    ptrC += 1

                if ptrA >= 0 and ptrC < nC:
                    best = min(best, startsC[ptrC] + lenC - startsA[ptrA])

            return best

        ans = INF

        if la == 0 and lb == 0 and lc == 0:
            return 0

        if la > 0:
            if lb > 0:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = three_part(A, la, B, lb, C, lc)
                else:
                    ans = two_part(A, la, B, lb)
            else:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = two_part(A, la, C, lc)
                else:
                    if A:
                        ans = la
        else:
            if lb > 0:
                if lc > 0:
                    ans = two_part(B, lb, C, lc)
                else:
                    if B:
                        ans = lb
            else:
                if lc > 0:
                    if C:
                        ans = lc

        return -1 if ans == INF else ans
```
